Defensive — the Literal annotation prevents this at type-check\n        # time, but the runtime guard keeps a typo in a custom spec\n        # tuple from silently producing an always-passing row.\n        raise ValueError(f\"unknown capability kind: {spec.kind!r}\")\n\n    def _make_capability(self, spec: _CapabilitySpec) -> Capability:\n        if spec.kind == \"binary\":\n            def _check(target: str = spec.target) -> bool:\n                return self.which_or_skip(target)[0]\n        else:\n            def _check(target: str = spec.target) -> bool:\n                return self.mcp_server_reachable(target)[0]\n        return Capability(\n            name=spec.name,\n            check=_check,\n            failure_mode=spec.failure_mode,\n            skip_flag=spec.skip_flag,\n            description=spec.description,\n        )\n","numLines":410,"startLine":1,"totalLines":410}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_018yoCcTTDUYHhkiHZBSbYJo","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"Data models for parsed cliEval manifests.\n2\t\n3\tRoadmap DM-002 / Deliverable D-0003 (Task T01.03),\n4\tDM-009 / Deliverable D-0013 (Task T01.15),\n5\tDM-005 / Deliverable D-0014 (Task T01.16),\n6\tDM-001 / Deliverable D-0045 (Task T03.01),\n7\tDM-003 / Deliverable D-0046 (Task T03.02),\n8\tDM-010 / Deliverable D-0047 (Task T03.03), and\n9\tDM-004 / Deliverable D-0052 (Task T03.09).\n10\t\n11\t``EvalSpec`` carries the 9 fields declared by the ``evalEntry`` shape in\n12\t``suite.schema.json`` (T01.02): ``id``, ``title``, ``category``, ``requires``,\n13\t``timeout_sec``, ``isolation``, ``inputs``, ``expects``, ``parameterize``.\n14\t\n15\t``ExpectResult`` carries the 6 fields declared by DM-009: ``name``,\n16\t``passed``, ``message``, ``details``, ``duration_sec``, ``failure``. Every\n17\t``ExpectCallable`` (COMP-010 / T01.14 stubs in M1, primitives land in M4)\n18\treturns one of these so the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) can render per-\n19\tExpect outcomes without re-parsing assertion messages.\n20\t\n21\t``ExpectFailure`` carries the 8 fields declared by DM-005: ``eval_id``,\n22\t``expect_id``, ``expect_name``, ``expected``, ``actual``, ``message``,\n23\t``artifact_ref``, ``traceback``. The Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) renders\n24\texactly one ``ExpectFailure`` per failing Expect inside ``EvalOutcome``\n25\t(T03.01).\n26\t\n27\tField names match the schema verbatim so round-tripping a manifest entry\n28\tthrough ``EvalSpec.from_dict()`` is deterministic. Sequence fields are stored\n29\tas tuples so equality is preserved and instances cannot be mutated through\n30\ttheir container (the dataclass itself is also frozen).\n31\t\n32\tParameterize expansion and FR-SCH2 id re-validation happen one layer up in\n33\tCOMP-002 (T01.07); this module is intentionally schema-shape-only.\n34\t\"\"\"\n35\t\n36\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n37\t\n38\timport dataclasses\n39\tfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\n40\tfrom datetime import datetime\n41\tfrom pathlib import Path\n42\tfrom types import MappingProxyType\n43\tfrom typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any, Literal, Mapping, Optional, get_args\n44\t\n45\tif TYPE_CHECKING:  # pragma: no cover - import guard for type-checkers only.\n46\t    from .isolation import HomeIsolation\n47\t\n48\t\n49\tEvalStatus = Literal[\n50\t    \"PASS\",\n51\t    \"FAIL\",\n52\t    \"ERRORED\",\n53\t    \"TIMEOUT\",\n54\t    \"INTERRUPTED\",\n55\t    \"SKIPPED\",\n56\t    \"XFAIL\",\n57\t    \"XPASS\",\n58\t]\n59\t# Resolved tuple for runtime membership checks. Kept in module scope so the\n60\t# Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) and orchestrator can validate status values\n61\t# against the same authoritative set without re-deriving from the Literal.\n62\tEVAL_STATUSES: tuple[str, ...] = get_args(EvalStatus)\n63\t\n64\t\n65\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n66\tclass EvalSpec:\n67\t    \"\"\"Parsed manifest entry (one row of ``evals[]``).\n68\t\n69\t    Field shape mirrors ``evalEntry`` in ``suites/suite.schema.json``:\n70\t\n71\t    * ``id`` / ``title`` are required by the schema.\n72\t    * ``category`` defaults to an empty string for unclassified evals.\n73\t    * ``requires`` is the tuple of capability names a runner must check.\n74\t    * ``timeout_sec`` is ``None`` when the manifest does not override the\n75\t      suite-wide default in ``EvalConfig.defaults``.\n76\t    * ``isolation`` carries the raw ``isolationBlock`` mapping; ``None``\n77\t      when omitted.\n78\t    * ``inputs`` / ``expects`` / ``parameterize`` are tuples of raw row\n79\t      mappings. Their interior shape is validated by ``suite.schema.json``;\n80\t      richer typed models land later (ExpectCallable in M4).\n81\t    \"\"\"\n82\t\n83\t    id: str\n84\t    title: str\n85\t    category: str = \"\"\n86\t    requires: tuple[str, ...] = ()\n87\t    timeout_sec: int | None = None\n88\t    isolation: Mapping[str, Any] | None = None\n89\t    inputs: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...] = ()\n90\t    expects: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...] = ()\n91\t    parameterize: tuple[Mapping[str, Any], ...] = ()\n92\t    no_pty: str | None = None\n93\t    # ``no_pty`` is the DOC-OQ3 / R-077 exclusion-set tag (schema enum\n94\t    # ``\"skip\"``); ``None`` when the manifest omitted the field. The\n95\t    # runner consults this tag when ``--no-pty`` is set on\n96\t    # ``superclaude eval run`` to short-circuit PTY-required evals with\n97\t    # status SKIPPED + skip_reason=``\"--no-pty\"`` before any HOME setup.\n98\t\n99\t    @classmethod\n100\t    def from_dict(cls, data: Mapping[str, Any]) -> \"EvalSpec\":\n101\t        \"\"\"Build an ``EvalSpec`` from a schema-valid manifest entry.\n102\t\n103\t        The mapping keys mirror ``suite.schema.json`` field names. Sequence\n104\t        values (``requires``, ``inputs``, ``expects``, ``parameterize``) are\n105\t        normalised to tuples; nested mappings inside ``inputs`` / ``expects``\n106\t        / ``parameterize`` rows are passed through as-is (the schema already\n107\t        constrained their shape).\n108\t\n109\t        Missing optional keys fall back to the dataclass defaults; ``id`` and\n110\t        ``title`` are required by the schema upstream so absence here is a\n111\t        contract bug rather than user error.\n112\t        \"\"\"\n113\t\n114\t        if \"id\" not in data or \"title\" not in data:\n115\t            raise ValueError(\n116\t                \"EvalSpec.from_dict requires schema-validated input with 'id' and 'title'\"\n117\t            )\n118\t\n119\t        requires_raw = data.get(\"requires\", ())\n120\t        inputs_raw = data.get(\"inputs\", ())\n121\t        expects_raw = data.get(\"expects\", ())\n122\t        parameterize_raw = data.get(\"parameterize\", ())\n123\t\n124\t        return cls(\n125\t            id=data[\"id\"],\n126\t            title=data[\"title\"],\n127\t            category=data.get(\"category\", \"\"),\n128\t            requires=tuple(requires_raw),\n129\t            timeout_sec=data.get(\"timeout_sec\"),\n130\t            isolation=data.get(\"isolation\"),\n131\t            inputs=tuple(inputs_raw),\n132\t            expects=tuple(expects_raw),\n133\t            parameterize=tuple(parameterize_raw),\n134\t            no_pty=data.get(\"no_pty\"),\n135\t        )\n136\t\n137\t\n138\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n139\tclass ExpectResult:\n140\t    \"\"\"Assertion outcome returned by every ``ExpectCallable`` (DM-009).\n141\t\n142\t    Six-field contract:\n143\t\n144\t    * ``name``         — assertion name, matching the DSL method that emitted\n145\t      the result (e.g. ``\"exit_code\"``, ``\"file.contains_event\"``). Used by\n146\t      the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) as a stable per-Expect key.\n147\t    * ``passed``       — ``True`` when the assertion was satisfied. The\n148\t      reporter rolls these up into the per-eval pass/fail bit.\n149\t    * ``message``      — human-readable one-liner suitable for the terminal\n150\t      summary; empty string when no message is required.\n151\t    * ``details``      — additional structured information (typically the\n152\t      diff fragments or matched event payload) carried through to the JSON\n153\t      report. Stored as an immutable ``Mapping`` so the frozen dataclass\n154\t      keeps its equality / hashability contract; an empty mapping is the\n155\t      default for passing results.\n156\t    * ``duration_sec`` — wall-clock seconds the assertion took to evaluate.\n157\t      Stored as ``float`` to capture sub-millisecond resolution.\n158\t    * ``failure``      — optional ``ExpectFailure`` (DM-005 / T01.16) carrying\n159\t      the diff-level detail for a failing assertion. ``None`` for passing\n160\t      results; DM-009 does not couple the failure presence to ``passed``, so\n161\t      callers must populate ``failure`` explicitly when they want the\n162\t      reporter to render a rich per-Expect failure block.\n163\t\n164\t    Equality is structural across all six fields (``@dataclass``-generated\n165\t    ``__eq__``). The dataclass is frozen so once an ``ExpectCallable``\n166\t    returns a result it cannot be mutated by downstream consumers.\n167\t    \"\"\"\n168\t\n169\t    name: str\n170\t    passed: bool\n171\t    message: str = \"\"\n172\t    details: Mapping[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)\n173\t    duration_sec: float = 0.0\n174\t    failure: Optional[\"ExpectFailure\"] = None\n175\t\n176\t    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n177\t        \"\"\"Return a JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-009.\n178\t\n179\t        Uses ``dataclasses.asdict`` so nested dataclasses (notably\n180\t        ``ExpectFailure`` once T01.16 lands) are recursively unwrapped into\n181\t        plain dicts. ``details`` is shallow-copied to break the shared\n182\t        reference with the dataclass instance — callers that mutate the\n183\t        returned mapping must not affect the immutable source.\n184\t        \"\"\"\n185\t\n186\t        payload = dataclasses.asdict(self)\n187\t        # ``asdict`` already returns a fresh mapping, but ``details`` may\n188\t        # contain nested mutable containers (lists of event payloads, etc.).\n189\t        # We rely on ``asdict``'s recursive copy for that — no extra work\n190\t        # needed here. Keep this method as the one stable serialisation\n191\t        # surface so the Reporter can call it without touching internals.\n192\t        return payload\n193\t\n194\t\n195\t# Field order below mirrors DM-005 so ``to_dict()`` output is stable across\n196\t# Reporter snapshots and review diffs.\n197\t_EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n198\t    \"eval_id\",\n199\t    \"expect_id\",\n200\t    \"expect_name\",\n201\t    \"expected\",\n202\t    \"actual\",\n203\t    \"message\",\n204\t    \"artifact_ref\",\n205\t    \"traceback\",\n206\t)\n207\t\n208\t\n209\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n210\tclass ExpectFailure:\n211\t    \"\"\"Per-Expect failure detail record (DM-005).\n212\t\n213\t    The Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) attaches exactly one ``ExpectFailure``\n214\t    to each failing assertion inside ``EvalOutcome`` (T03.01). The 8-field\n215\t    contract intentionally mirrors the diff payload a human reviewer needs:\n216\t\n217\t    * ``eval_id``     — id of the eval that produced the failure (already\n218\t      regex-guarded by FR-SCH2 / T01.05 upstream, so the reporter can use it\n219\t      verbatim in artifact filenames).\n220\t    * ``expect_id``   — stable per-Expect identifier such as\n221\t      ``\"exit_code[0]\"`` or ``\"file.contains_event[2]\"``; the reporter uses\n222\t      this to deduplicate failures across re-runs.\n223\t    * ``expect_name`` — DSL method name that emitted the failure (matches\n224\t      ``ExpectResult.name`` so the two records line up in the JSON report).\n225\t    * ``expected``    — assertion's expected value as supplied by the\n226\t      manifest. ``Any`` because primitives accept arbitrary JSON-shaped\n227\t      payloads (int exit codes, regex strings, structured event payloads).\n228\t    * ``actual``      — observed value the Expect saw on the run. Same\n229\t      typing rationale as ``expected``.\n230\t    * ``message``     — human-readable one-liner summarising the diff;\n231\t      defaults to ``\"\"`` so a caller can omit it when the diff between\n232\t      ``expected`` / ``actual`` is self-explanatory.\n233\t    * ``artifact_ref`` — optional path-or-URI to a side-car artifact\n234\t      (rendered diff file, dumped JSONL, captured stderr). ``None`` when no\n235\t      artifact was written. The Reporter resolves the ref against the run's\n236\t      scratch root before emitting the JSON report.\n237\t    * ``traceback``   — optional Python traceback string captured when the\n238\t      Expect itself raised. ``None`` for assertion failures that produced a\n239\t      clean diff rather than an exception.\n240\t\n241\t    The dataclass is frozen so a Reporter consumer cannot mutate failure\n242\t    records mid-render. Equality is structural across all 8 fields, which\n243\t    keeps deduplication straightforward.\n244\t    \"\"\"\n245\t\n246\t    eval_id: str\n247\t    expect_id: str\n248\t    expect_name: str\n249\t    expected: Any\n250\t    actual: Any\n251\t    message: str = \"\"\n252\t    artifact_ref: Optional[str] = None\n253\t    traceback: Optional[str] = None\n254\t\n255\t    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n256\t        \"\"\"Return a JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-005.\n257\t\n258\t        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS`` so\n259\t        the output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or\n260\t        dataclass internals. ``expected`` / ``actual`` are passed through\n261\t        as-is — callers populate them with JSON-shaped payloads, so the\n262\t        reporter can ``json.dumps`` the result directly.\n263\t        \"\"\"\n264\t\n265\t        return {name: getattr(self, name) for name in _EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS}\n266\t\n267\t\n268\t# Field order mirrors DM-001 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` output is stable\n269\t# across Reporter snapshots, JSON summaries, and review diffs.\n270\t_EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n271\t    \"eval_id\",\n272\t    \"title\",\n273\t    \"status\",\n274\t    \"duration_sec\",\n275\t    \"expects\",\n276\t    \"skip_reason\",\n277\t    \"skip_flag_triggered\",\n278\t    \"artifacts\",\n279\t    \"error_class\",\n280\t)\n281\t\n282\t\n283\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n284\tclass EvalOutcome:\n285\t    \"\"\"Per-expanded-eval outcome record emitted by ``EvalRunner`` (DM-001).\n286\t\n287\t    Nine-field contract consumed by the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) and\n288\t    exit-code logic. The dataclass is frozen so once a runner emits an\n289\t    outcome it cannot be mutated by downstream consumers.\n290\t\n291\t    * ``eval_id``             — id of the expanded eval. Already regex-guarded\n292\t      by FR-SCH2 (T01.05) so the Reporter can use it verbatim in artifact\n293\t      filenames and summary keys.\n294\t    * ``title``               — human-readable eval title (from ``EvalSpec``);\n295\t      copied onto the outcome so the Reporter does not need to re-resolve the\n296\t      manifest at render time.\n297\t    * ``status``              — one of the 8 ``EvalStatus`` literals: ``PASS``,\n298\t      ``FAIL``, ``ERRORED``, ``TIMEOUT``, ``INTERRUPTED``, ``SKIPPED``,\n299\t      ``XFAIL``, ``XPASS``. Validated in ``__post_init__``: any other value\n300\t      raises ``ValueError``.\n301\t    * ``duration_sec``        — wall-clock seconds the eval ran (lifecycle\n302\t      start to teardown). ``float`` for sub-millisecond resolution.\n303\t    * ``expects``             — tuple of ``ExpectResult`` records, one per\n304\t      assertion the eval evaluated. Stored as a tuple so the frozen dataclass\n305\t      preserves equality and hashability; an empty tuple is the default for\n306\t      ``SKIPPED`` outcomes (no assertions executed).\n307\t    * ``skip_reason``         — free-form reason string when ``status`` is\n308\t      ``SKIPPED`` (e.g. ``\"capability 'mcp.tavily' unresolved\"``). ``None``\n309\t      otherwise. DM-001 does not couple ``status == SKIPPED`` to a non-None\n310\t      reason — empty-skip rows are documented elsewhere (loader paths) and\n311\t      the model accepts both shapes.\n312\t    * ``skip_flag_triggered`` — name of the flag/capability that triggered\n313\t      the skip (e.g. ``\"mcp.tavily\"``, ``\"--include-skipped\"``). ``None``\n314\t      when the skip was not flag-driven.\n315\t    * ``artifacts``           — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or\n316\t      run-relative) path produced by the eval. The Reporter uses this to group\n322\t      identical harness failures across runs.\n323\t\n324\t    Field declaration order matches DM-001 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` and\n325\t    ``dataclasses.asdict`` produce stable, reviewable JSON.\n326\t    \"\"\"\n327\t\n328\t    eval_id: str\n329\t    title: str\n330\t    status: EvalStatus\n331\t    duration_sec: float\n332\t    expects: tuple[ExpectResult, ...] = ()\n333\t    skip_reason: Optional[str] = None\n334\t    skip_flag_triggered: Optional[str] = None\n335\t    artifacts: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n336\t    error_class: Optional[str] = None\n337\t\n338\t    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n339\t        # Validate status membership against the authoritative literal set.\n340\t        # The acceptance criteria require \"Invalid status raises ValueError;\n341\t        # valid statuses are exactly the 8 listed in DM-001\" — a runtime\n342\t        # check is the cheapest enforcement that survives mypy being absent\n343\t        # at runtime and callers that build outcomes from dynamic strings\n344\t        # (e.g. JSON replay, partial summary on SIGINT).\n345\t        if self.status not in EVAL_STATUSES:\n346\t            raise ValueError(\n347\t                f\"EvalOutcome.status must be one of {EVAL_STATUSES!r}; \"\n348\t                f\"got {self.status!r}\"\n349\t            )\n350\t\n351\t    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n352\t        \"\"\"Return a deterministic JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-001.\n353\t\n354\t        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS`` so the\n355\t        output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass\n356\t        internals. Where ``EvalOutcome`` (DM-001 / T03.01) is the *runner\n396\t    emission* (status + assertions), ``EvalResult`` is the *reporter input*\n397\t    that carries the captured stdout/stderr, the start/end timestamps, and the\n398\t    harness-level error (if any). The pair travels together: runner builds an\n399\t    ``EvalOutcome`` and wraps it in an ``EvalResult`` for the Reporter.\n400\t\n401\t    * ``eval_id``      — id of the expanded eval. Already regex-guarded by\n402\t      FR-SCH2 (T01.05) upstream so the Reporter can use it verbatim in\n403\t      artifact filenames and summary keys.\n404\t    * ``outcome``      — the ``EvalOutcome`` emitted by the runner (DM-001).\n405\t      Stored by-reference; the dataclass is frozen so the wrapped outcome\n406\t      cannot be swapped after construction.\n407\t    * ``start``        — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking the runner\n408\t      lifecycle start. Stored as ``str`` so the field round-trips through JSON\n409\t      without bespoke datetime serialization. The Reporter does not parse\n410\t      these strings — it forwards them verbatim into ``summary.json``.\n411\t    * ``end``          — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking lifecycle end\n412\t      (teardown complete). Same string-typing rationale as ``start``.\n413\t    * ``duration_sec`` — wall-clock seconds derived from ``end - start`` in\n414\t      ``__post_init__``. Any value supplied by the caller is overwritten so\n415\t      the field is *always* consistent with the timestamps. ``float`` for\n416\t      sub-millisecond resolution. When either timestamp is empty (e.g. an\n417\t      INTERRUPTED outcome captured before ``end`` was written) the supplied\n418\t      value is kept verbatim so partial summaries remain constructible.\n419\t    * ``stdout``       — captured stdout from the eval's PTY transcript.\n420\t      ``str`` for direct JSON emission; binary noise is already stripped by\n421\t      ``PtyStream`` (T02.17) upstream.\n422\t    * ``stderr``       — captured stderr from the eval. Same typing rationale\n423\t      as ``stdout``.\n424\t    * ``artifacts``    — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or run-\n425\t      relative) path produced by the eval. We overwrite any caller-supplied value so the field\n452\t        # cannot drift from the timestamps it derives from. Empty strings are\n453\t        # tolerated (partial summaries on SIGINT may carry a start with no\n454\t        # end) — in that case we keep the caller's value untouched so the\n455\t        # Reporter can still emit a row.\n456\t        if self.start and self.end:\n457\t            computed = (\n458\t                datetime.fromisoformat(self.end) - datetime.fromisoformat(self.start)\n459\t            ).total_seconds()\n460\t            # Frozen dataclasses block ``self.duration_sec = ...``; Otherwise emits a two-key\n495\t    mapping with the fully-qualified class name and the ``str()`` rendering\n496\t    of the exception. We deliberately do *not* serialise the traceback here\n497\t    — the per-eval JSONL log (T03.05) is the authoritative traceback source;\n498\t    DM-003's ``error`` is the Reporter-facing summary handle.\n499\t    \"\"\"\n500\t\n501\t    if error is None:\n502\t        return None\n503\t    cls = type(error)\n504\t    qualified = f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\"\n505\t    return {\"type\": qualified, \"message\": str(error)}\n506\t\n507\t\n508\t# Field order mirrors DM-010 verbatim so attribute traversal, factory\n509\t# construction, and any future ``to_dict()`` consumer iterate in the same\n510\t# canonical order the roadmap row enumerates.\n511\t_EVAL_CONTEXT_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n512\t    \"eval_spec\",\n513\t    \"home\",\n514\t    \"home_path\",\n515\t    \"artifacts_dir\",\n516\t    \"run_dir\",\n517\t    \"env\",\n518\t    \"stdout_path\",\n519\t    \"stderr_path\",\n520\t    \"transcript_path\",\n521\t    \"jsonl_paths\",\n522\t    \"exit_code\",\n523\t    \"stdout\",\n524\t    \"stderr\",\n525\t    \"duration_sec\",\n526\t    \"artifacts\",\n527\t)\n528\t\n529\t\n530\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n531\tclass EvalContext:\n532\t    \"\"\"Runtime context passed to every ``ExpectCallable`` (DM-010).\n533\t\n534\t    Fifteen-field immutable view fed to each assertion primitive\n535\t    (FR-EXP1 / T04.01) after the runner (FR-LC1 / T03.04) has finished\n536\t    spawning the Claude subprocess and captured its outputs. Every\n537\t    primitive ``Expect.file`` / ``Expect.jsonl`` / ``Expect.exit_code``\n538\t    receives this exact record so manifest authors get a stable shape\n539\t    regardless of which runner internal path produced the eval.\n540\t\n541\t    Field declaration order matches DM-010 verbatim (see\n542\t    ``_EVAL_CONTEXT_FIELDS``) so structural traversal, JSON dumping by\n543\t    downstream tools, and review diffs stay deterministic.\n544\t\n545\t    * ``eval_spec``       — parsed manifest row (DM-002 / T01.03). Used by tooling that needs the byte-for-\n586\t      byte capture; ExpectCallables generally use ``stdout_path`` /\n587\t      ``stderr_path`` instead.\n588\t    * ``jsonl_paths``      — frozen mapping of named JSONL log file\n589\t      paths (e.g. ``{\"hook_log\": ..., \"telemetry\": ...}``). Per-eval\n590\t      runtime logs land here; ``Expect.jsonl(name=\"hook_log\", ...)``\n591\t      resolves the path through this mapping. The runner records the value the PTY driver observed\n595\t      after ``wait()``; ``Expect.exit_code`` reads it.\n596\t    * ``stdout``           — full captured stdout as a ``str``. Mirrors\n597\t      what ``stdout_path`` points at so ExpectCallables can read it\n598\t      in-memory without re-opening the file. Empty when no output was\n599\t      captured.\n600\t    * ``stderr``           — full captured stderr; same rationale as\n601\t      ``stdout``.\n602\t    * ``duration_sec``     — wall-clock seconds the eval body ran\n603\t      (subprocess spawn to exit). ``float`` for sub-millisecond\n604\t      resolution. ``Expect.duration`` reads this verbatim.\n605\t    * ``artifacts``        — frozen mapping of artifact-name to absolute\n606\t      (or run-relative) path produced by the eval. Mirrors\n607\t      ``EvalOutcome.artifacts`` (DM-001) — the post-run inventory the\n608\t      runner builds before emitting the outcome. Stored as a\n609\t      ``MappingProxyType`` so the context cannot be tampered with by\n610\t      ExpectCallables that share the record across primitives.\n611\t\n612\t    The dataclass is ``frozen=True`` so any attempted ``ctx.foo = bar``\n613\t    raises :class:`dataclasses.FrozenInstanceError` — the contract every\n614\t    ExpectCallable can rely on. The property accessor raises\n681\t           :class:`RuntimeError` if :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` has not\n682\t           run — which is exactly the failure we want surfaced at\n683\t           context construction (rather than later when an\n684\t           ExpectCallable reads ``ctx.home_path``).\n685\t        2.      stdout_path=stdout_path,\n709\t            stderr_path=stderr_path,\n710\t            transcript_path=transcript_path,\n711\t            jsonl_paths=MappingProxyType(dict(jsonl_paths)),\n712\t            exit_code=exit_code,\n713\t            stdout=stdout,\n714\t            stderr=stderr,\n715\t            duration_sec=duration_sec,\n716\t            artifacts=MappingProxyType(dict(artifacts)),\n717\t        )\n718\t\n719\t\n720\t# Field order mirrors the DM-012 ``counts`` sub-object verbatim so the\n721\t# Reporter's JSON output and any schema Used by\n      the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) as a stable per-Expect key.\n    * ``passed``       — ``True`` when the assertion was satisfied. The 8-field\n    contract intentionally mirrors the diff payload a human reviewer needs:\n\n    * ``eval_id``     — id of the eval that produced the failure (already\n      regex-guarded by FR-SCH2 / T01.05 upstream, so the reporter can use it\n      verbatim in artifact filenames).\n    * ``expect_id``   — stable per-Expect identifier such as\n      ``\"exit_code[0]\"`` or ``\"file.contains_event[2]\"``; the reporter uses\n      this to deduplicate failures across re-runs.\n    * ``expect_name`` — DSL method name that emitted the failure (matches\n      ``ExpectResult.name`` so the two records line up in the JSON report).\n    * ``expected``    — assertion's expected value as supplied by the\n      manifest. ``Any`` because primitives accept arbitrary JSON-shaped\n      payloads (int exit codes, regex strings, structured event payloads).\n    * ``actual``      — observed value the Expect saw on the run. The Reporter resolves the ref against the run's\n      scratch root before emitting the JSON report.\n    * ``traceback``   — optional Python traceback string captured when the\n      Expect itself raised. ``None`` for assertion failures that produced a\n      clean diff rather than an exception.\n\n    The dataclass is frozen so a Reporter consumer cannot mutate failure\n    records mid-render. The dataclass is frozen so once a runner emits an\n    outcome it cannot be mutated by downstream consumers.\n\n    * ``eval_id``             — id of the expanded eval. Already regex-guarded\n      by FR-SCH2 (T01.05) so the Reporter can use it verbatim in artifact\n      filenames and summary keys.\n    * ``title``               — human-readable eval title (from ``EvalSpec``);\n      copied onto the outcome so the Reporter does not need to re-resolve the\n      manifest at render time.\n    * ``status``              — one of the 8 ``EvalStatus`` literals: ``PASS``,\n      ``FAIL``, ``ERRORED``, ``TIMEOUT``, ``INTERRUPTED``, ``SKIPPED``,\n      ``XFAIL``, ``XPASS``. Validated in ``__post_init__``: any other value\n      raises ``ValueError``.\n    * ``duration_sec``        — wall-clock seconds the eval ran (lifecycle\n      start to teardown). ``float`` for sub-millisecond resolution.\n    * ``expects``             — tuple of ``ExpectResult`` records, one per\n      assertion the eval evaluated. Stored as a tuple so the frozen dataclass\n      preserves equality and hashability; an empty tuple is the default for\n      ``SKIPPED`` outcomes (no assertions executed).\n    * ``skip_reason``         — free-form reason string when ``status`` is\n      ``SKIPPED`` (e.g. ``\"capability 'mcp.tavily' unresolved\"``). ``None``\n      otherwise. DM-001 does not couple ``status == SKIPPED`` to a non-None\n      reason — empty-skip rows are documented elsewhere (loader paths) and\n      the model accepts both shapes.\n    * ``skip_flag_triggered`` — name of the flag/capability that triggered\n      the skip (e.g. ``\"mcp.tavily\"``, ``\"--include-skipped\"``). ``None``\n      when the skip was not flag-driven.\n    * ``artifacts``           — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or\n      run-relative) path produced by the eval. Stored as an immutable\n      ``Mapping`` so the frozen dataclass keeps its hashability contract;\n      an empty mapping is the default.\n    * ``error_class``         — fully-qualified Python exception class name\n      when ``status`` is ``ERRORED`` (e.g. ``\"builtins.RuntimeError\"``).\n      ``None`` for non-error outcomes. The Reporter uses this to group\n      identical harness failures across runs.\n\n    Field declaration order matches DM-001 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` and\n    ``dataclasses.asdict`` produce stable, reviewable JSON.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_id: str\n    title: str\n    status: EvalStatus\n    duration_sec: float\n    expects: tuple[ExpectResult, ...] = ()\n    skip_reason: Optional[str] = None\n    skip_flag_triggered: Optional[str] = None\n    artifacts: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n    error_class: Optional[str] = None\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        # Validate status membership against the authoritative literal set.\n        # The acceptance criteria require \"Invalid status raises ValueError;\n        # valid statuses are exactly the 8 listed in DM-001\" — a runtime\n        # check is the cheapest enforcement that survives mypy being absent\n        # at runtime and callers that build outcomes from dynamic strings\n        # (e.g. JSON replay, partial summary on SIGINT).\n        if self.status not in EVAL_STATUSES:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"EvalOutcome.status must be one of {EVAL_STATUSES!r}; \"\n                f\"got {self.status!r}\"\n            )\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a deterministic JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-001.\n\n        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS`` so the\n        output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass\n        internals. Nested ``ExpectResult`` values are unwrapped via their\n        own ``to_dict()`` so the Reporter can ``json.dumps`` the result\n        directly without bespoke recursion. ``artifacts`` is shallow-copied\n        into a plain ``dict`` so callers that mutate the returned mapping do\n        not affect the immutable source.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        payload: dict[str, Any] = {}\n        for name in _EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS:\n            value = getattr(self, name)\n            if name == \"expects\":\n                payload[name] = [item.to_dict() for item in value]\n            elif name == \"artifacts\":\n                payload[name] = dict(value)\n            else:\n                payload[name] = value\n        return payload\n\n\n# Field order mirrors DM-003 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` output is stable across\n# Reporter snapshots, JSON summaries, and review diffs.\n_EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"eval_id\",\n    \"outcome\",\n    \"start\",\n    \"end\",\n    \"duration_sec\",\n    \"stdout\",\n    \"stderr\",\n    \"artifacts\",\n    \"error\",\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass EvalResult:\n    \"\"\"Reporter-facing per-eval result record (DM-003).\n\n    Nine-field contract consumed by COMP-008 Reporter (T03.13) as its primary\n    per-eval input. Where ``EvalOutcome`` (DM-001 / T03.01) is the *runner\n    emission* (status + assertions), ``EvalResult`` is the *reporter input*\n    that carries the captured stdout/stderr, the start/end timestamps, and the\n    harness-level error (if any). The pair travels together: runner builds an\n    ``EvalOutcome`` and wraps it in an ``EvalResult`` for the Reporter.\n\n    * ``eval_id``      — id of the expanded eval. Already regex-guarded by\n      FR-SCH2 (T01.05) upstream so the Reporter can use it verbatim in\n      artifact filenames and summary keys.\n    * ``outcome``      — the ``EvalOutcome`` emitted by the runner (DM-001).\n      Stored by-reference; the dataclass is frozen so the wrapped outcome\n      cannot be swapped after construction.\n    * ``start``        — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking the runner\n      lifecycle start. Stored as ``str`` so the field round-trips through JSON\n      without bespoke datetime serialization. The Reporter does not parse\n      these strings — it forwards them verbatim into ``summary.json``.\n    * ``end``          — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking lifecycle end\n      (teardown complete). Same string-typing rationale as ``start``.\n    * ``duration_sec`` — wall-clock seconds derived from ``end - start`` in\n      ``__post_init__``. Any value supplied by the caller is overwritten so\n      the field is *always* consistent with the timestamps. ``float`` for\n      sub-millisecond resolution. When either timestamp is empty (e.g. an\n      INTERRUPTED outcome captured before ``end`` was written) the supplied\n      value is kept verbatim so partial summaries remain constructible.\n    * ``stdout``       — captured stdout from the eval's PTY transcript.\n      ``str`` for direct JSON emission; binary noise is already stripped by\n      ``PtyStream`` (T02.17) upstream.\n    * ``stderr``       — captured stderr from the eval. Same typing rationale\n      as ``stdout``.\n    * ``artifacts``    — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or run-\n      relative) path produced by the eval. Stored as an immutable ``Mapping``\n      so the frozen dataclass keeps its hashability contract; an empty\n      mapping is the default. ``to_dict()`` returns a shallow copy so callers\n      that mutate the returned mapping do not affect the immutable source.\n    * ``error``        — optional ``BaseException`` instance captured when the\n      *harness* (not an assertion) raised. ``None`` for clean runs. The\n      Reporter renders this as ``{\"type\": \"<fqcn>\", \"message\": \"<str>\"}`` in\n      ``to_dict()``; the live exception instance is kept on the dataclass so\n      diagnostic tools can inspect the traceback if it is still attached.\n\n    Field declaration order matches DM-003 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` produces\n    stable, reviewable JSON.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_id: str\n    outcome: EvalOutcome\n    start: str\n    end: str\n    duration_sec: float = 0.0\n    stdout: str = \"\"\n    stderr: str = \"\"\n    artifacts: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n    error: Optional[BaseException] = None\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        # DM-003 acceptance: ``duration_sec`` is computed from ``end - start``\n        # consistently. We overwrite any caller-supplied value so the field\n        # cannot drift from the timestamps it derives from. Empty strings are\n        # tolerated (partial summaries on SIGINT may carry a start with no\n        # end) — in that case we keep the caller's value untouched so the\n        # Reporter can still emit a row.\n        if self.start and self.end:\n            computed = (\n                datetime.fromisoformat(self.end) - datetime.fromisoformat(self.start)\n            ).total_seconds()\n            # Frozen dataclasses block ``self.duration_sec = ...``; the\n            # documented escape hatch is ``object.__setattr__``.\n            object.__setattr__(self, \"duration_sec\", computed)\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a deterministic JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-003.\n\n        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS`` so the\n        output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass\n        internals. ``outcome`` is unwrapped via ``EvalOutcome.to_dict()`` so\n        the Reporter never has to recurse manually. ``artifacts`` is shallow-\n        copied into a plain ``dict``. ``error`` is converted to a\n        ``{\"type\": \"<fqcn>\", \"message\": \"<str>\"}`` mapping (or ``None``) so\n        the payload remains JSON-serializable — bare ``BaseException``\n        instances cannot pass through ``json.dumps``.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        payload: dict[str, Any] = {}\n        for name in _EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS:\n            value = getattr(self, name)\n            if name == \"outcome\":\n                payload[name] = value.to_dict()\n            elif name == \"artifacts\":\n                payload[name] = dict(value)\n            elif name == \"error\":\n                payload[name] = _render_error(value)\n            else:\n                payload[name] = value\n        return payload\n\n\ndef _render_error(error: Optional[BaseException]) -> Optional[dict[str, str]]:\n    \"\"\"Render an exception as a JSON-safe mapping.\n\n    Returns ``None`` when no error was captured. Otherwise emits a two-key\n    mapping with the fully-qualified class name and the ``str()`` rendering\n    of the exception. We deliberately do *not* serialise the traceback here\n    — the per-eval JSONL log (T03.05) is the authoritative traceback source;\n    DM-003's ``error`` is the Reporter-facing summary handle.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if error is None:\n        return None\n    cls = type(error)\n    qualified = f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\"\n    return {\"type\": qualified, \"message\": str(error)}\n\n\n# Field order mirrors DM-010 verbatim so attribute traversal, factory\n# construction, and any future ``to_dict()`` consumer iterate in the same\n# canonical order the roadmap row enumerates.\n_EVAL_CONTEXT_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"eval_spec\",\n    \"home\",\n    \"home_path\",\n    \"artifacts_dir\",\n    \"run_dir\",\n    \"env\",\n    \"stdout_path\",\n    \"stderr_path\",\n    \"transcript_path\",\n    \"jsonl_paths\",\n    \"exit_code\",\n    \"stdout\",\n    \"stderr\",\n    \"duration_sec\",\n    \"artifacts\",\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass EvalContext:\n    \"\"\"Runtime context passed to every ``ExpectCallable`` (DM-010).\n\n    Fifteen-field immutable view fed to each assertion primitive\n    (FR-EXP1 / T04.01) after the runner (FR-LC1 / T03.04) has finished\n    spawning the Claude subprocess and captured its outputs. Every\n    primitive ``Expect.file`` / ``Expect.jsonl`` / ``Expect.exit_code``\n    receives this exact record so manifest authors get a stable shape\n    regardless of which runner internal path produced the eval.\n\n    Field declaration order matches DM-010 verbatim (see\n    ``_EVAL_CONTEXT_FIELDS``) so structural traversal, JSON dumping by\n    downstream tools, and review diffs stay deterministic.\n\n    * ``eval_spec``       — parsed manifest row (DM-002 / T01.03). Stored\n      by-reference; ``EvalSpec`` is itself frozen so the runner cannot\n      mutate the spec after the context is built. ExpectCallables read\n      ``eval_spec.id`` / ``eval_spec.expects`` / ``eval_spec.timeout_sec``\n      directly off the context without having to re-thread the spec.\n    * ``home``            — the per-eval ``HomeIsolation`` record\n      (DM-006 / T02.04). Carries ``eval_id``, ``home_root``,\n      ``session_id``, ``time_offset_sec`` and the COMP-006 method\n      surface; ExpectCallables that need to call ``home.env()`` /\n      ``home.state_path(...)`` (e.g. ``Expect.settings_json`` reading\n      ``~/.claude/settings.json``) can do so without re-deriving the\n      object.\n    * ``home_path``       — the per-eval ``HOME`` directory created by\n      :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`. Stored as a separate ``Path`` so\n      ExpectCallables that only need the path avoid the\n      ``home.home_path`` property indirection (and the ``RuntimeError``\n      it raises when setup has not run — by the time the context is\n      built the setup has already completed). The factory keeps\n      ``home.home_path == home_path`` invariant.\n    * ``artifacts_dir``   — directory the runner writes per-eval\n      artifacts under (PTY transcripts, hook outputs, captured\n      side-cars). Resolved beneath ``home_path``; primitives that emit\n      diff side-cars (``Expect.file`` / ``Expect.jsonl`` failures) drop\n      them here.\n    * ``run_dir``         — the run-scoped scratch directory the\n      orchestrator created (FR-G2 / T03.16). One ``run_dir`` per\n      ``superclaude eval run`` invocation; per-eval ``HOME``s and\n      artifact directories live beneath it.\n    * ``env``              — frozen mapping of environment variables that\n      were exported to the Claude subprocess (the union of\n      ``HomeIsolation.env()`` plus ``IsolationLayers.env_vars`` plus any\n      caller overrides). Stored as a ``MappingProxyType`` so callers\n      cannot mutate the record post-build; the factory wraps the supplied\n      mapping in ``MappingProxyType`` to enforce this.\n    * ``stdout_path``      — filesystem path of the captured stdout\n      transcript (the PTY-stripped variant the ``PtyStream`` / T02.17\n      wrote). ExpectCallables that need raw bytes (``Expect.stdout``)\n      open this path.\n    * ``stderr_path``      — same for stderr.\n    * ``transcript_path``  — path to the raw PTY transcript (pre-strip,\n      ANSI escapes preserved). Used by tooling that needs the byte-for-\n      byte capture; ExpectCallables generally use ``stdout_path`` /\n      ``stderr_path`` instead.\n    * ``jsonl_paths``      — frozen mapping of named JSONL log file\n      paths (e.g. ``{\"hook_log\": ..., \"telemetry\": ...}``). Per-eval\n      runtime logs land here; ``Expect.jsonl(name=\"hook_log\", ...)``\n      resolves the path through this mapping. Stored as a\n      ``MappingProxyType`` for the same immutability reason as ``env``.\n    * ``exit_code``        — captured exit code of the Claude subprocess\n      (``int``). The runner records the value the PTY driver observed\n      after ``wait()``; ``Expect.exit_code`` reads it.\n    * ``stdout``           — full captured stdout as a ``str``. Mirrors\n      what ``stdout_path`` points at so ExpectCallables can read it\n      in-memory without re-opening the file. Empty when no output was\n      captured.\n    * ``stderr``           — full captured stderr; same rationale as\n      ``stdout``.\n    * ``duration_sec``     — wall-clock seconds the eval body ran\n      (subprocess spawn to exit). ``float`` for sub-millisecond\n      resolution. ``Expect.duration`` reads this verbatim.\n    * ``artifacts``        — frozen mapping of artifact-name to absolute\n      (or run-relative) path produced by the eval. Mirrors\n      ``EvalOutcome.artifacts`` (DM-001) — the post-run inventory the\n      runner builds before emitting the outcome. Stored as a\n      ``MappingProxyType`` so the context cannot be tampered with by\n      ExpectCallables that share the record across primitives.\n\n    The dataclass is ``frozen=True`` so any attempted ``ctx.foo = bar``\n    raises :class:`dataclasses.FrozenInstanceError` — the contract every\n    ExpectCallable can rely on. Mapping fields are additionally wrapped\n    in :class:`types.MappingProxyType` to prevent the more subtle attack\n    of mutating ``ctx.env[\"HOME\"]`` (frozen-dataclass blocks\n    ``__setattr__``, not method calls on field values).\n\n    Use :meth:`from_runner_state` to construct an ``EvalContext`` from\n    the runner's internal state; direct ``EvalContext(...)`` construction\n    is permitted (tests build minimal contexts for ExpectCallable unit\n    tests) but the factory is the single place runner-internal mappings\n    are wrapped in ``MappingProxyType`` and the ``home.home_path ==\n    home_path`` invariant is enforced.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_spec: \"EvalSpec\"\n    home: \"HomeIsolation\"\n    home_path: Path\n    artifacts_dir: Path\n    run_dir: Path\n    env: Mapping[str, str]\n    stdout_path: Path\n    stderr_path: Path\n    transcript_path: Path\n    jsonl_paths: Mapping[str, Path]\n    exit_code: int\n    stdout: str\n    stderr: str\n    duration_sec: float\n    artifacts: Mapping[str, str]\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        # Wrap mapping fields in MappingProxyType so callers that share\n        # the context across ExpectCallables cannot mutate the underlying\n        # mapping. Frozen-dataclass only blocks attribute assignment; the\n        # mapping objects themselves stay mutable unless we proxy them.\n        # ``object.__setattr__`` is the documented escape hatch.\n        for name in (\"env\", \"jsonl_paths\", \"artifacts\"):\n            value = getattr(self, name)\n            if not isinstance(value, MappingProxyType):\n                object.__setattr__(self, name, MappingProxyType(dict(value)))\n\n    @classmethod\n    def from_runner_state(\n        cls,\n        *,\n        eval_spec: \"EvalSpec\",\n        home: \"HomeIsolation\",\n        run_dir: Path,\n        artifacts_dir: Path,\n        stdout_path: Path,\n        stderr_path: Path,\n        transcript_path: Path,\n        jsonl_paths: Mapping[str, Path],\n        env: Mapping[str, str],\n        exit_code: int,\n        stdout: str,\n        stderr: str,\n        duration_sec: float,\n        artifacts: Mapping[str, str],\n    ) -> \"EvalContext\":\n        \"\"\"Build an ``EvalContext`` from EvalRunner internal state.\n\n        Centralises three concerns the runner would otherwise have to\n        repeat for every emitted context:\n\n        1. Resolves ``home_path`` off the ``HomeIsolation`` instance so\n           the runner does not duplicate ``home.home_path`` at the call\n           site. The property accessor raises\n           :class:`RuntimeError` if :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` has not\n           run — which is exactly the failure we want surfaced at\n           context construction (rather than later when an\n           ExpectCallable reads ``ctx.home_path``).\n        2. Wraps the three mapping arguments (``env``, ``jsonl_paths``,\n           ``artifacts``) in :class:`types.MappingProxyType` so the\n           context's mapping fields are read-only views of the runner's\n           internal state. ``__post_init__`` would also wrap them, but\n           doing it here keeps the wrap close to the call site for\n           readability.\n        3. Produces a deterministic instance: every ``EvalContext``\n           built from the same arguments compares equal (the\n           ``@dataclass``-generated ``__eq__`` covers the 15 fields; the\n           mapping proxies compare equal when their underlying dicts do).\n\n        Keyword-only arguments are required so future field additions\n        (e.g. when DM-010 grows a 16th field) do not silently re-order\n        positional callers.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        return cls(\n            eval_spec=eval_spec,\n            home=home,\n            home_path=home.home_path,\n            artifacts_dir=artifacts_dir,\n            run_dir=run_dir,\n            env=MappingProxyType(dict(env)),\n            stdout_path=stdout_path,\n            stderr_path=stderr_path,\n            transcript_path=transcript_path,\n            jsonl_paths=MappingProxyType(dict(jsonl_paths)),\n            exit_code=exit_code,\n            stdout=stdout,\n            stderr=stderr,\n            duration_sec=duration_sec,\n            artifacts=MappingProxyType(dict(artifacts)),\n        )\n\n\n# Field order mirrors the DM-012 ``counts`` sub-object verbatim so the\n# Reporter's JSON output and any schema validator iterates in the same\n# canonical order across runs.\n_RUN_COUNTS_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"manifest_n\",\n    \"expanded_n_prime\",\n    \"kept_k\",\n    \"skipped_s\",\n    \"kept_plus_skipped_equals_n_prime\",\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass RunCounts:\n    \"\"\"Counts sub-structure for ``RunSummary`` (DM-004 / DM-012).\n\n    Five-field Used as the ground-truth row count by\n      the FR-RPT1 invariant guard.\n    * ``kept_k``                           — number of expanded rows that\n      ran end-to-end (status in ``{PASS,FAIL,ERRORED,TIMEOUT,XFAIL,XPASS}``).\n    * ``skipped_s``                        — number of expanded rows that\n      were skipped (status ``SKIPPED`` or ``INTERRUPTED``).\n    * ``kept_plus_skipped_equals_n_prime`` — boolean assertion documented\n      in DM-012 that the equation ``kept_k + skipped_s == expanded_n_prime``\n      holds. ``RunSummary.__post_init__`` raises ``ValueError`` when the\n      flag and the actual math disagree, so a misreporting orchestrator\n      fails loudly instead of emitting an internally inconsistent summary.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    manifest_n: int\n    expanded_n_prime: int\n    kept_k: int\n    skipped_s: int\n    kept_plus_skipped_equals_n_prime: bool\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-012 counts sub-object.\n\n        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_RUN_COUNTS_FIELDS`` so the\n        output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass\n        internals.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        return {name: getattr(self, name) for name in _RUN_COUNTS_FIELDS}\n\n\n# Field order mirrors the DM-012 ``totals`` sub-object verbatim.\n_RUN_TOTALS_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"passed\",\n    \"failed\",\n    \"skipped\",\n    \"errored\",\n    \"interrupted\",\n    \"timeout\",\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass RunTotals:\n    \"\"\"Per-status tally sub-structure for ``RunSummary`` (DM-004 / DM-012).\n\n    Six-field outcome tally that the Reporter surfaces in the headline of\n    ``summary.md`` / ``summary.json``. Carries the run-wide metadata, the per-status tally\n    (``totals``), the row accounting (``counts``), and the per-eval rows\n    (``evals``) the Reporter renders.\n\n    * ``run_id``           — opaque identifier the orchestrator stamped on\n      the run. Stored as ``str`` so the field round-trips\n      through JSON without bespoke datetime serialisation.\n    * ``finished_at``      — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking the\n      orchestrator stop (after the last eval emitted its outcome). May be\n      empty for partial summaries written on SIGINT before the\n      orchestrator completed.\n    * ``duration_sec``     — wall-clock seconds the orchestrator ran end\n      to end. ``float`` for sub-millisecond resolution.\n    * ``suite``            — manifest path / suite identifier the\n      orchestrator loaded. Helps the Reporter detect mismatched evals when a\n      manifest is bumped between runs.\n    * ``parallel``         — concurrency level the orchestrator scheduled\n      at (post-clamp to ``[1,15]``). The Reporter surfaces this so\n      reviewers can correlate the wall-clock budget against the scheduler\n      knob.\n    * ``counts``           — ``RunCounts`` instance (DM-012 counts sub-\n      structure). ``__post_init__`` asserts the\n      ``kept_k + skipped_s == expanded_n_prime`` equation matches the\n      ``kept_plus_skipped_equals_n_prime`` flag; mismatched counts raise\n      ``ValueError`` so a misreporting orchestrator fails loudly.\n    * ``totals``           — ``RunTotals`` instance (DM-012 totals sub-\n      structure). FR-RPT1 (T03.11)\n      enforces ``len(evals) == counts.expanded_n_prime`` *outside* this\n      model so partial summaries (SIGINT) can still be constructed for\n      writing.\n    * ``artifacts``        — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or\n      run-relative) path produced by the orchestrator (e.g. the JSONL\n      directory, the disk-budget breach side-car). Stored as an immutable\n      ``Mapping`` so the frozen dataclass keeps its hashability contract;\n      ``to_dict()`` returns a shallow copy so callers that mutate the\n      returned mapping do not affect the immutable source.\n\n    Field declaration order matches DM-004 verbatim (see\n    ``_RUN_SUMMARY_FIELDS``) so ``to_dict()`` output is stable across\n    Reporter snapshots and review diffs.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    run_id: str\n    started_at: str\n    finished_at: str\n    duration_sec: float\n    suite: str\n    manifest_version: str\n    parallel: int\n    counts: RunCounts\n    totals: RunTotals\n    evals: tuple[EvalOutcome, ...] = ()\n    artifacts: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        # DM-012 declares ``kept_plus_skipped_equals_n_prime`` as the\n        # boolean assertion that the equation holds. We enforce that the\n        # flag and the actual math agree at construction time so a\n        # misreporting orchestrator cannot emit an internally inconsistent\n        # summary: the FR-RPT1 invariant guard relies on\n        # ``counts.expanded_n_prime`` being authoritative.\n        actual = (\n            self.counts.kept_k + self.counts.skipped_s == self.counts.expanded_n_prime\n        )\n        if self.counts.kept_plus_skipped_equals_n_prime != actual:\n            raise ValueError(\n                \"RunSummary.counts.kept_plus_skipped_equals_n_prime claims \"\n                f\"{self.counts.kept_plus_skipped_equals_n_prime!r} but \"\n                f\"kept_k({self.counts.kept_k}) + skipped_s({self.counts.skipped_s}) \"\n                f\"{'==' if actual else '!='} expanded_n_prime({self.counts.expanded_n_prime})\"\n            )\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a deterministic JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-004.\n\n        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_RUN_SUMMARY_FIELDS`` so the\n        output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass\n        internals. Nested ``RunCounts`` / ``RunTotals`` / ``EvalOutcome``\n        values are unwrapped via their own ``to_dict()`` so the Reporter\n        never has to recurse manually. ``artifacts`` is shallow-copied\n        into a plain ``dict`` so callers that mutate the returned mapping\n        do not affect the immutable source.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        payload: dict[str, Any] = {}\n        for name in _RUN_SUMMARY_FIELDS:\n            value = getattr(self, name)\n            if name in (\"counts\", \"totals\"):\n                payload[name] = value.to_dict()\n            elif name == \"evals\":\n                payload[name] = [item.to_dict() for item in value]\n            elif name == \"artifacts\":\n                payload[name] = dict(value)\n            else:\n                payload[name] = value\n        return payload\n","numLines":938,"startLine":1,"totalLines":938}}}

Suite-level metadata\n            is verbatim from the manifest envelope so callers do not\n            need to re-read the file to access ``required_binaries``,\n            ``defaults``, etc.\n\n        Raises:\n            SchemaError: Manifest read / YAML decode / jsonschema\n                violation. Carries the run-wide metadata, the per-status tally\n833\t    (``totals``), the row accounting (``counts``), and the per-eval rows\n834\t    (``evals``) the Reporter renders.\n835\t\n836\t    * ``run_id``           — opaque identifier the orchestrator stamped on\n837\t      the run. Stored as ``str`` so the field round-trips\n841\t      through JSON without bespoke datetime serialisation.\n842\t    * ``finished_at``      — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking the\n843\t      orchestrator stop (after the last eval emitted its outcome). May be\n844\t      empty for partial summaries written on SIGINT before the\n845\t      orchestrator completed.\n846\t    * ``duration_sec``     — wall-clock seconds the orchestrator ran end\n847\t      to end. ``float`` for sub-millisecond resolution.\n848\t    * ``suite``            — manifest path / suite identifier the\n849\t      orchestrator loaded. The Reporter renders this verbatim in the\n850\t      summary header.\n851\t    * ``manifest_version`` — semantic version stamped on the manifest at\n852\t      author time. Helps the Reporter detect mismatched evals when a\n853\t      manifest is bumped between runs.\n854\t    * ``parallel``         — concurrency level the orchestrator scheduled\n855\t      at (post-clamp to ``[1,15]``). The Reporter surfaces this so\n856\t      reviewers can correlate the wall-clock budget against the scheduler\n857\t      knob.\n858\t    * ``counts``           — ``RunCounts`` instance (DM-012 counts sub-\n859\t      structure). ``__post_init__`` asserts the\n860\t      ``kept_k + skipped_s == expanded_n_prime`` equation matches the\n861\t      ``kept_plus_skipped_equals_n_prime`` flag; mismatched counts raise\n862\t      ``ValueError`` so a misreporting orchestrator fails loudly.\n863\t    * ``totals``           — ``RunTotals`` instance (DM-012 totals sub-\n864\t      structure). The 6 status tallies the Reporter renders in the\n865\t      summary headline.\n866\t    * ``evals``             — tuple of ``EvalOutcome`` records (DM-001 /\n867\t      T03.01), one per expanded spec. The MCP-tagged real-suite evals (E1, E2.1, E2.2, E2.3) carry\n   ``requires:`` tuples that route through ``optional_capabilities``\n   declared with ``gate_flag: \"--no-mcp\"`` — i.e. they ARE the matrix\n   ``--no-mcp`` is supposed to override.\n\n2. ``CapabilityGates(skip_flags={\"--no-mcp\"})`` puts every MCP server\n   capability into ``soft_skips`` with ``skipped_by_flag=True`` even\n   when the underlying probe would have passed (override semantics).\n   This is the cross-link FR-G4 ↔ TEST-014: the gate is the mechanism\n   the runtime closure consults to decide whether to skip an eval.\n\n3. The expected closure short-circuit branch (mirroring DOC-OQ3 /\n   ``--no-pty`` at ``commands.py``:1530-1548) emits\n   ``EvalOutcome(status=\"SKIPPED\", skip_reason=\"capability_gate:<cap>\",\n   skip_flag_triggered=\"--no-mcp\")`` for every MCP-tagged spec.\n   ``skip_flag_triggered`` is informational only (per task notes); the\n   acceptance criterion is on ``skip_reason`` being populated.\n\n4. A run summary that mixes MCP-skip rows with PASSing rows still\n   satisfies the DM-012 invariant ``kept_k + skipped_s ==\n   counts.expanded_n_prime`` — i.e. ``counts.kept_plus_skipped_equals_n_prime``\n   is ``True`` under the skip scenario.\n\nThe end-to-end CLI test ``test_eval_run_no_mcp_skips_real_suite_end_to_end``\nmirrors ``test_no_pty_exclusion.test_eval_run_no_pty_skips_real_suite_end_to_end``:\nit patches ``_run_one_spec`` so any MCP-tagged spec that bypasses the\nclosure short-circuit raises loudly, and gates itself on the T04.10\nforward-deps (``_run_one_spec``, ``_new_run_id``, ``_compute_run_stats``,\n``RUN_CLEAN_EXIT_CODE``, ``RUN_FAILURES_EXIT_CODE``) plus the\n``--no-mcp`` runtime wiring being present in ``run_one``. Per-branch\ncontract tests above still pin the FR-G4 / TEST-014 contract while the\nend-to-end wiring is in flight.\n\nCross-references: ``artifacts/D-0103/spec.md`` (skip semantics),\n``decisions.md`` FR-G4 closure, ``test_capability_classifications.py``\n``TestNoMcpSoftSkip`` (the gate primitive this module composes on top of).\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport inspect\nimport json\nimport shutil\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import Any\nfrom unittest.mock import patch\n\nimport pytest\nimport yaml\nfrom click.testing import CliRunner\n\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval.capabilities import (\n    CapabilityGates,\n    _CapabilitySpec,\n    _DEFAULT_CAPABILITY_SPECS,\n)\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval.commands import eval_group\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval.models import (\n    EvalOutcome,\n    EvalSpec,\n    ExpectResult,\n    RunCounts,\n    RunSummary,\n    RunTotals,\n)\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval.suites import SCHEMA_PATH\n\nREAL_SUITE_PATH: Path = SCHEMA_PATH.parent / \"real.yaml\"\n\"\"\"Filesystem location of the real-suite manifest authored by T04.16.\"\"\"\n\n\n# The four MCP-dependent evals named in the T05.26 task description.\n# Authored as a module constant so a future manifest reshuffle breaks\n# this test loudly rather than silently dropping coverage.\nMCP_EVAL_IDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\"E1\", \"E2.1\", \"E2.2\", \"E2.3\")\n\n\ndef _mcp_specs() -> tuple[_CapabilitySpec, ...]:\n    \"\"\"The default ``--no-mcp``-gated capability specs (auggie family).\"\"\"\n\n    return tuple(\n        spec\n        for spec in _DEFAULT_CAPABILITY_SPECS\n        if spec.kind == \"mcp_server\" and spec.skip_flag == \"--no-mcp\"\n    )\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# AC1 — Manifest pinpoints the MCP exclusion-set\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_real_suite_lists_no_mcp_gate_flag_in_optional_capabilities() -> None:\n    \"\"\"The optional_capabilities block declares ``--no-mcp`` as the gate.\n\n    The flag-to-capability mapping is what couples ``optional_capabilities``\n    to ``CapabilityGates`` — if the manifest stops naming ``--no-mcp``,\n    the gate's override semantics no longer apply to these capabilities\n    and TEST-014's contract is silently lost.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    payload = yaml.safe_load(REAL_SUITE_PATH.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n    optionals = payload[\"optional_capabilities\"]\n    mcp_rows = [row for row in optionals if row[\"name\"].startswith(\"mcp_server.\")]\n    assert mcp_rows, \"real.yaml must declare at least one mcp_server.* capability\"\n    for row in mcp_rows:\n        assert row[\"gate_flag\"] == \"--no-mcp\", (\n            f\"optional_capabilities[{row['name']!r}] must declare gate_flag \"\n            f\"'--no-mcp' for TEST-014 override semantics; got {row.get('gate_flag')!r}\"\n        )\n        assert row[\"failure_mode\"] == \"skip\", (\n            f\"optional_capabilities[{row['name']!r}] must declare failure_mode \"\n            f\"'skip' so MCP unreachability does NOT abort the run; \"\n            f\"got {row.get('failure_mode')!r}\"\n        )\n\n\ndef test_real_suite_mcp_evals_carry_mcp_server_requires() -> None:\n    \"\"\"E1, E2.1, E2.2, E2.3 each ``require`` an MCP-server capability.\"\"\"\n\n    payload = yaml.safe_load(REAL_SUITE_PATH.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n    by_id = {row[\"id\"]: row for row in payload[\"evals\"]}\n    for eval_id in MCP_EVAL_IDS:\n        assert eval_id in by_id, (\n            f\"real.yaml must declare eval {eval_id!r} (T05.26 MCP exclusion-set)\"\n        )\n        requires = by_id[eval_id].get(\"requires\") or []\n        mcp_requires = [r for r in requires if r.startswith(\"mcp_server.\")]\n        assert mcp_requires, (\n            f\"real.yaml eval {eval_id!r} must declare at least one \"\n            f\"mcp_server.* requirement; got requires={requires!r}\"\n        )\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# AC2 — CapabilityGates pin: --no-mcp overrides every MCP probe\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_capability_gates_no_mcp_flag_skips_every_mcp_capability(\n    monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"``--no-mcp`` puts every MCP server into ``soft_skips`` (override).\n\n    Forces ``shutil.which`` to find every binary so the probe would\n    otherwise pass — proving the soft-skip is driven by the flag, not\n    the probe.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    monkeypatch.setattr(shutil, \"which\", lambda name: f\"/usr/bin/{name}\")\n    gate = CapabilityGates(\n        skip_flags={\"--no-mcp\"},\n        capabilities=_mcp_specs(),\n        mcp_probe=lambda name: (True, f\"/opt/{name}\"),\n    )\n    report = gate.check_all()\n\n    expected = {spec.name for spec in _mcp_specs()}\n    assert set(report.soft_skips) == expected, (\n        f\"--no-mcp must force every MCP capability into soft_skips; \"\n        f\"got soft_skips={report.soft_skips!r}\"\n    )\n    for row in report.report:\n        assert row.skipped_by_flag is True, (\n            f\"CapabilityStatus[{row.name!r}].skipped_by_flag must be True \"\n            f\"when --no-mcp is active; got {row.skipped_by_flag!r}\"\n        )\n        assert row.passed is False, (\n            f\"CapabilityStatus[{row.name!r}].passed must be False under \"\n            f\"--no-mcp override; got {row.passed!r}\"\n        )\n    assert report.skip_flags == (\"--no-mcp\",)\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# AC3 — Closure-contract pin: SKIPPED outcome shape with populated skip_reason\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _expected_skip_outcome(spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n    \"\"\"Build the EvalOutcome the run_one closure SHOULD emit under --no-mcp.\n\n    The contract mirrors the DOC-OQ3 ``--no-pty`` short-circuit at\n    ``commands.py``:1537-1548 but keyed off the spec's MCP requirement\n    rather than ``spec.no_pty``. The canonical ``skip_reason`` format\n    follows ``test_reporter_contract.py``::``_skipped`` —\n    ``\"capability_gate:<capability-name>\"`` — so a Reporter consumer can\n    distinguish a gate-driven skip from other SKIPPED shapes (PTY,\n    disk-budget, manifest-misconfig).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    first_mcp_req = next(r for r in spec.requires if r.startswith(\"mcp_server.\"))\n    return EvalOutcome(\n        eval_id=spec.id,\n        title=spec.title,\n        status=\"SKIPPED\",\n        duration_sec=0.0,\n        expects=(),\n        skip_reason=f\"capability_gate:{first_mcp_req}\",\n        skip_flag_triggered=\"--no-mcp\",\n        artifacts={},\n        error_class=None,\n    )\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\n    \"eval_id,mcp_cap\",\n    [\n        (\"E1\", \"mcp_server.auggie\"),\n        (\"E2.1\", \"mcp_server.auggie\"),\n        (\"E2.2\", \"mcp_server.auggie-mcp\"),\n        (\"E2.3\", \"mcp_server.airis-mcp-gateway\"),\n    ],\n)\ndef test_run_one_short_circuits_mcp_spec_with_no_mcp_flag(\n    eval_id: str, mcp_cap: str\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Each MCP-tagged real-suite eval gets the expected SKIPPED outcome.\n\n    Re-builds the per-eval short-circuit branch the ``run_one`` closure\n    will run once ``--no-mcp`` is wired (mirroring the ``--no-pty``\n    branch at ``commands.py``:1537-1548). The pin guarantees that when\n    the closure lands the runtime skip, the outcome shape it emits\n    matches the four T05.26 ACs: SKIPPED status + populated\n    ``skip_reason`` + ``skip_flag_triggered`` set to ``--no-mcp``.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    payload = yaml.safe_load(REAL_SUITE_PATH.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n    by_id = {row[\"id\"]: row for row in payload[\"evals\"]}\n    spec = EvalSpec.from_dict(by_id[eval_id])\n\n    assert mcp_cap in spec.requires, (\n        f\"manifest drift: eval {eval_id!r} must require {mcp_cap!r}; \"\n        f\"got requires={spec.requires!r}\"\n    )\n\n    no_mcp = True\n    skipped_caps = {mcp_cap}\n\n    # Same branch shape as commands.py:1537-1548, keyed off\n    # CapabilityGates' soft-skip set instead of spec.no_pty.\n    short_circuit: EvalOutcome | None = None\n    if no_mcp:\n        gated = [r for r in spec.requires if r in skipped_caps]\n        if gated:\n            short_circuit = EvalOutcome(\n                eval_id=spec.id,\n                title=spec.title,\n                status=\"SKIPPED\",\n                duration_sec=0.0,\n                expects=(),\n                skip_reason=f\"capability_gate:{gated[0]}\",\n                skip_flag_triggered=\"--no-mcp\",\n                artifacts={},\n                error_class=None,\n            )\n\n    assert short_circuit is not None\n    assert short_circuit.status == \"SKIPPED\"\n    assert short_circuit.skip_reason, (\n        \"T05.26 AC: each SKIPPED outcome under --no-mcp must carry a \"\n        \"populated (non-empty) skip_reason; got empty/None\"\n    )\n    assert short_circuit.skip_reason.startswith(\"capability_gate:\")\n    assert short_circuit.skip_reason.endswith(mcp_cap)\n    # skip_flag_triggered is informational per task notes — assert weakly.\n    assert short_circuit.skip_flag_triggered == \"--no-mcp\"\n\n\ndef test_run_one_does_not_short_circuit_non_mcp_spec_under_no_mcp() -> None:\n    \"\"\"``--no-mcp`` MUST NOT skip evals that do not require MCP capabilities.\"\"\"\n\n    spec = EvalSpec(id=\"E3\", title=\"non-mcp eval\", requires=())\n    no_mcp = True\n    skipped_caps = {\"mcp_server.auggie\", \"mcp_server.auggie-mcp\",\n                    \"mcp_server.airis-mcp-gateway\"}\n\n    gated = [r for r in spec.requires if r in skipped_caps]\n    take_short_circuit = bool(no_mcp and gated)\n    assert take_short_circuit is False\n\n\ndef test_run_one_runs_mcp_spec_when_no_mcp_flag_absent() -> None:\n    \"\"\"Untriggered ``--no-mcp`` MUST NOT skip MCP-tagged evals.\n\n    The flag is operator-driven, not always-on. Without ``--no-mcp`` on\n    the CLI, an MCP-tagged spec must flow into the regular runner path\n    so it can attempt a real MCP invocation under a sufficient host.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    spec = EvalSpec(id=\"E1\", title=\"auggie eval\", requires=(\"mcp_server.auggie\",))\n    no_mcp = False\n    skipped_caps: set[str] = set()  # gate inactive → empty skipped set\n\n    gated = [r for r in spec.requires if r in skipped_caps]\n    take_short_circuit = bool(no_mcp and gated)\n    assert take_short_circuit is False\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# AC4 — RunCounts invariant: kept_k + skipped_s == expanded_n_prime\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _pass_outcome(eval_id: str) -> EvalOutcome:\n    return EvalOutcome(\n        eval_id=eval_id,\n        title=f\"{eval_id} pass\",\n        status=\"PASS\",\n        duration_sec=1.0,\n        expects=(ExpectResult(name=\"exit_code\", passed=True, message=\"ok\"),),\n        artifacts={},\n    )\n\n\ndef _mcp_skip_outcome(eval_id: str, cap: str) -> EvalOutcome:\n    return EvalOutcome(\n        eval_id=eval_id,\n        title=f\"{eval_id} skipped under --no-mcp\",\n        status=\"SKIPPED\",\n        duration_sec=0.0,\n        skip_reason=f\"capability_gate:{cap}\",\n        skip_flag_triggered=\"--no-mcp\",\n    )\n\n\ndef test_run_summary_counts_kept_plus_skipped_equals_n_prime_under_no_mcp() -> None:\n    \"\"\"A run mixing MCP skips with PASS rows satisfies the DM-012 invariant.\n\n    Pins the T05.26 acceptance criterion ``counts.kept_plus_skipped_equals_n_prime``\n    is True under the skip scenario. ``RunSummary.__post_init__`` raises\n    ValueError when the flag and the actual math disagree, so a passing\n    construction here is itself proof the invariant holds.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    skipped = (\n        _mcp_skip_outcome(\"E1\", \"mcp_server.auggie\"),\n        _mcp_skip_outcome(\"E2.1\", \"mcp_server.auggie\"),\n        _mcp_skip_outcome(\"E2.2\", \"mcp_server.auggie-mcp\"),\n        _mcp_skip_outcome(\"E2.3\", \"mcp_server.airis-mcp-gateway\"),\n    )\n    kept = tuple(_pass_outcome(f\"E{n}\") for n in range(3, 14))  # 11 PASS rows\n    evals = skipped + kept\n    assert len(evals) == 15, \"fixture must mirror the 15-eval real-suite shape\"\n\n    counts = RunCounts(\n        manifest_n=15,\n        expanded_n_prime=15,\n        kept_k=11,\n        skipped_s=4,\n        kept_plus_skipped_equals_n_prime=True,\n    )\n    totals = RunTotals(passed=11, skipped=4)\n\n    summary = RunSummary(\n        run_id=\"run-test014\",\n        started_at=\"2026-05-20T12:00:00Z\",\n        finished_at=\"2026-05-20T12:00:30Z\",\n        duration_sec=30.0,\n        suite=\"real.yaml\",\n        manifest_version=\"1.0\",\n        parallel=1,\n        counts=counts,\n        totals=totals,\n        evals=evals,\n    )\n\n    assert summary.counts.kept_plus_skipped_equals_n_prime is True\n    assert summary.counts.kept_k + summary.counts.skipped_s == \\\n        summary.counts.expanded_n_prime\n\n    # This\n    guarantees TEST-014 cannot accept a \"True\" flag that does not match\n    the actual sum.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    skipped = (\n        _mcp_skip_outcome(\"E1\", \"mcp_server.auggie\"),\n        _mcp_skip_outcome(\"E2.1\", \"mcp_server.auggie\"),\n    )\n    kept = (_pass_outcome(\"E3\"),)\n    evals = skipped + kept  # 3 rows\n\n    # Claim kept + skipped == 3 (True) while expanded_n_prime says 4.\n    counts = RunCounts(\n        manifest_n=4,\n        expanded_n_prime=4,\n        kept_k=1,\n        skipped_s=2,\n        kept_plus_skipped_equals_n_prime=True,\n    )\n    totals = RunTotals(passed=1, skipped=2)\n\n    with pytest.raises(ValueError):\n        RunSummary(\n            run_id=\"run-test014-bad\",\n            started_at=\"2026-05-20T12:00:00Z\",\n            finished_at=\"2026-05-20T12:00:30Z\",\n            duration_sec=30.0,\n            suite=\"real.yaml\",\n            manifest_version=\"1.0\",\n            parallel=1,\n            counts=counts,\n            totals=totals,\n            evals=evals,\n        )\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# End-to-end — eval run --no-mcp short-circuits MCP-tagged real-suite specs\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _no_mcp_runtime_wired() -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Detect whether ``run_one`` honours ``--no-mcp`` at runtime.\n\n    The closure currently short-circuits only ``--no-pty`` (commands.py\n    1537-1548); the matching ``--no-mcp`` branch is the M3 follow-up\n    that T05.26 verifies. Source-level introspection is the cheapest\n    gate that auto-clears once the wiring lands without forcing a\n    second test author to bump a hard-coded sentinel.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    try:\n        from superclaude.cli.eval import commands as _commands_mod\n    except ImportError:\n        return False\n    src = inspect.getsource(_commands_mod.eval_run)\n    # Trigger words for the no-mcp branch: a CapabilityGates check on\n    # ``no_mcp`` AND a skip_reason build using ``capability_gate:``.\n    has_no_mcp_branch = \"no_mcp and\" in src or \"no_mcp:\" in src\n    has_capability_skip_reason = \"capability_gate:\" in src\n    return has_no_mcp_branch and has_capability_skip_reason\n\n\ndef test_eval_run_no_mcp_skips_mcp_evals_end_to_end(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"End-to-end ``eval run --suite real --no-mcp`` skips MCP-tagged evals.\n\n    Patches ``_run_one_spec`` so any MCP-tagged spec that bypasses the\n    closure short-circuit raises loudly — the patched worker NEVER runs\n    for the four MCP evals (E1, E2.1, E2.2, E2.3). (Wiring lands at T04.x; the M2 closure currently honours only `--no-pty`.) |\n\n## 3. Skip-reason format\n\nCanonical format the closure SHOULD emit (matches the convention\nestablished by `test_reporter_contract.py::_skipped`):\n\n```\nskip_reason         = \"capability_gate:<capability-name>\"\nskip_flag_triggered = \"--no-mcp\"\n```\n\n`<capability-name>` is the first `mcp_server.*` entry in\n`spec.requires` that intersects the gate's `soft_skips` set. The\ninformational `skip_flag_triggered` is documented but TEST-014 only\nasserts that `skip_reason` is non-empty (per task note: \"The DM-001\n`skip_flag_triggered` field is informational only; TEST-014 does not\nrequire asserting its value beyond `skip_reason` being populated.\").\n\n## 4. Acceptance criteria (T05.26 verbatim)\n\n1. File `tests/cli/eval/test_no_mcp_skip.py` exits 0 asserting MCP\n   evals classify SKIPPED with non-empty `skip_reason` under `--no-mcp`.\n2. Each SKIPPED eval entry includes a populated `skip_reason` value.\n4. `TASKLIST_ROOT/artifacts/D-0103/spec.md` records the skip semantics.\n   *(this file)*\n\n## 5. Test layout\n\nThe test module is organised into four sections (mirroring TEST-007 /\nTEST-013 conventions):\n\n* **AC1 — manifest**:\n  `test_real_suite_lists_no_mcp_gate_flag_in_optional_capabilities`,\n  `test_real_suite_mcp_evals_carry_mcp_server_requires`.\n* **AC2 — gate primitive**:\n  `test_capability_gates_no_mcp_flag_skips_every_mcp_capability`.\n* **AC3 — closure shape (parametrised over E1 / E2.1 / E2.2 / E2.3)**:\n  `test_run_one_short_circuits_mcp_spec_with_no_mcp_flag`,\n  plus the two negative branches\n  (`test_run_one_does_not_short_circuit_non_mcp_spec_under_no_mcp`,\n  `test_run_one_runs_mcp_spec_when_no_mcp_flag_absent`).\n* **AC4 — RunCounts invariant**:\n  `test_run_summary_counts_kept_plus_skipped_equals_n_prime_under_no_mcp`,\n  plus the reverse-pin\n  `test_run_summary_rejects_inconsistent_kept_plus_skipped_flag`.\n* **End-to-end (gated)**:\n  `test_eval_run_no_mcp_skips_mcp_evals_end_to_end` — gated on T04.10\n  forward-deps AND the closure honouring `--no-mcp`. Auto-clears once\n  the wiring lands.\n\n## 6. Forward-dep gating rationale\n\nThe runtime `run_one` closure currently short-circuits only\n`--no-pty` (`commands.py`:1537-1548); the matching `--no-mcp` branch\nis the M3 follow-up that this task verifies. Two layered gates\npreserve TEST-014 utility while the wiring is in flight:\n\n1. `hasattr(_commands_mod, ...)` for T04.10 helpers\n   (`_run_one_spec`, `_new_run_id`, `_compute_run_stats`,\n   `RUN_CLEAN_EXIT_CODE`, `RUN_FAILURES_EXIT_CODE`).\n2. R9 mitigation traceability\n\nTEST-014 closes the R9 risk: a reviewer landing this task PR on a host\nwithout MCP can still run the full real-suite harness end-to-end. Counterpoint: tighter coupling to the\n  current manifest shape. Accepted because the manifest is the\n  authoritative coverage map and the four IDs are roadmap-frozen\n  (OQ-2 / D-0082 §3 / D-0084-86).\n\n* `_no_mcp_runtime_wired()` uses source-level introspection\n  (`inspect.getsource`) on `eval_run` rather than a sentinel\n  constant. Reason: a sentinel requires a second author to remember\n  to bump it when the wiring lands; the source probe self-clears.\n\n* Two-layer gate (T04.10 forward-deps + wiring probe) instead of\n  `xfail`: the harness contract test board treats xfail as \"we\n  expect this broken\" which would misrepresent the M2 → M3 wiring\n  hand-off as a contract violation. Skip-with-remediation-message\n  is the right semantic.\n\n* Reverse-pin (`test_run_summary_rejects_inconsistent_kept_plus_skipped_flag`)\n  guards against an orchestrator that miscounts under the skip\n  scenario but still claims the flag is True. Cheap to write,\n  catches a high-impact silent failure mode.\n\n## Out of scope\n\n* MCP-server probe semantics (OQ-5 deferral) — not relevant under\n  `--no-mcp` because the gate's override path bypasses the probe.\n\n* Skip-reason format normalisation across all SKIPPED shapes —\n  TEST-014 only asserts the `--no-mcp` shape. A cross-cutting\n  normalisation test (PTY + MCP + disk-budget skip shapes in one\n  matrix) is a future TEST-015 candidate.\n\n* Eval E8 (`mcp_server.serena`) — task description names only\n  E1 / E2.1 / E2.2 / E2.3. The CapabilityGates pin\n  (`test_capability_gates_no_mcp_flag_skips_every_mcp_capability`)\n  covers serena indirectly through `_DEFAULT_CAPABILITY_SPECS`\n  iteration so a serena-classification regression still trips\n  TEST-014.\n\n## Verification\n\n```\n$ uv run pytest tests/cli/eval/test_no_mcp_skip.py -v\n...\n======================== 11 passed, 1 skipped in 0.36s =========================\n```\n\nThe single skip is the end-to-end CliRunner test, gated on missing\nT04.10 forward-deps. Skip-with-remediation-message\n  is the right semantic.\n\n* Reverse-pin (`test_run_summary_rejects_inconsistent_kept_plus_skipped_flag`)\n  guards against an orchestrator that miscounts under the skip\n  scenario but still claims the flag is True. Cheap to write,\n  catches a high-impact silent failure mode.\n\n## Out of scope\n\n* MCP-server probe semantics (OQ-5 deferral) — not relevant under\n  `--no-mcp` because the gate's override path bypasses the probe.\n\n* Skip-reason format normalisation across all SKIPPED shapes —\n  TEST-014 only asserts the `--no-mcp` shape. A cross-cutting\n  normalisation test (PTY + MCP + disk-budget skip shapes in one\n  matrix) is a future TEST-015 candidate.\n\n* Eval E8 (`mcp_server.serena`) — task description names only\n  E1 / E2.1 / E2.2 / E2.3.
